**Italians** ([Italian](14708.md): *italiani*, pronounced [\[itaˈljaːni\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Italian)) are an [ethnic group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_group) and [nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation) native to the [Italian geographical region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_geographical_region).[^cite_note-Minahan-48] Italians share a common [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_culture), [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Italy), [ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_heritage) and [language](14708.md). Their ancestors, differing regionally, include the various [Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_peoples_of_Italy), notably among them the [ancient Romans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_people), who helped create and evolve the Italian identity.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-istat.it-51][^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53][^cite_note-54] The Latin equivalent of the [term Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Name_of_Italy) had been in use for natives of the [geographical region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_geographical_region) since [antiquity](252905.md).[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] Ethnic Italians (a group which includes people of Italian descent without Italian citizenship) can be distinguished from [Italian nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_nationality_law), who are citizens of [Italy](14532.md) regardless of ancestry or nation of residence.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61]

The majority of Italian nationals are native speakers of the country's official language, [Standard Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Italian), a [Romance language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romance_languages) of the [Indo-European language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_languages) that evolved from the [Vulgar Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulgar_Latin). However, many of them also speak a [regional or minority language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_or_minority_language) native to Italy, the existence of which predates the national language of Standard Italian.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-worldatlas-63] (According to [UNESCO](21786641.md), there are approximately 30 [languages native to Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Italy), although many are often incorrectly referred to as "Italian [dialects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialects)".)[^cite_note-istat-64][^cite_note-istat.it-51][^cite_note-maiden-65][^cite_note-unesco-66]

In addition to the approximately 55 million Italians living in Italy (91% of the Italian national population),[^cite_note-Instat-1][^cite_note-67] Italian-speaking groups are found in neighboring nations, including [Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_Switzerland),[^cite_note-68] [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_France),[^cite_note-69] the regions of [Istria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istria) and [Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatia), and the entire population of [San Marino](27248.md). Due to the wide-ranging [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_diaspora) of Italians following [Italian unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_unification), [World War I](4764461.md), and [World War II](32927.md), over 5 million Italian citizens live outside of Italy[^cite_note-70] and over 80 million people around the world claim full or partial Italian ancestry.[^cite_note-italians2010-71] The largest Italian diaspora communities are found in [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilians) (15% of [Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilians)),[^cite_note-Brazil-72] [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentines) (60% of [Argentinians](18951905.md)),[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans), and [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Canadians).

Italians have influenced and contributed to fields like [arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_art) and [music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Italy), [science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science), [technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology), [fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fashion), [cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_cinema), [cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_cuisine), [restaurants](26437.md), [sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport), [jurisprudence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurisprudence), [banking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank) and [business](39206.md).[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76][^cite_note-77][^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] Furthermore, Italian people are generally known for their attachment to their locale, expressed in the form of either [regionalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regionalism_\(politics\)) or [municipalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parochialism).[^cite_note-80]

## Name

Further information: [Name of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Name_of_Italy)



Silver coin minted in [Corfinium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corfinium) during the [Social War (91–87 BC)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_War_\(91%E2%80%9387_BC\)), displaying the inscription *ITALIA* on the verge of the [personification of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italia_turrita), represented as a goddess with [laurel wreath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurel_wreath)

Wiktionary logo

Look up ***[Italian](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Italian)*** or ***[italiano](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/italiano)*** in Wiktionary, the free dictionary.

The Latin name "Italia" may have been borrowed via [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek) from the [Oscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscan_language) *Víteliú* ("land of calves").[^cite_note-Manco-81][^cite_note-82] Accounts by [Dionysius of Halicarnassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dionysius_of_Halicarnassus),[^cite_note-83] [Aristotle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristotle),[^cite_note-84] and [Thucydides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thucydides)[^cite_note-85] reference this etymology, together with the legend that Italy was named after legendary [King Italus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italus). According to [Antiochus of Syracuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiochus_of_Syracuse), the Greeks initially used the term Italy to refer only to the southern portion of the [Bruttium peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Italy) (corresponding to parts of the modern provinces of [Reggio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reggio_Calabria), [Catanzaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catanzaro), and [Vibo Valentia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vibo_Valentia)); however, over time, the Greeks gradually came to apply the name "Italia" to a larger region including [Lucania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucania) and [Calabria](44772.md).[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87]

Roman historian [Cato the Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cato_the_Elder) described Italy as the entire peninsula south of the [Alps](981.md), which he said formed the "walls of Italy".[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] In the 260s BCE, Roman Italy extended from the [Arno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arno) and [Rubicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubicon) rivers to the entire south. The northern area of [Cisalpine Gaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisalpine_Gaul) was occupied by Rome in the 220s BC and became considered geographically and *[de facto](43594.md)* part of Italy,[^cite_note-90] but remained politically and *[de jure](43594.md)* separated until [Octavian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octavian) legally merged it into the administrative unit of Italy in 42 BCE.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-DRR-92][^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94][^cite_note-au-95] Under Emperor [Diocletian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocletian), Italy was further enlarged to include the three big islands of the western [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md): [Sicily](27619.md) (with the [Maltese archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maltese_archipelago)), [Sardinia](29376.md), and [Corsica](5714828.md).[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-Treccani-97] All its inhabitants were considered *Italic* and *Roman*.[^cite_note-StraboneItaliaV1.1-98]

The Latin term *Italicus* was used to describe "a man of Italy" as opposed to a [provincial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_province) from the greater Roman provinces.[^cite_note-99] The Greeks likewise used terms such as *Ἰταλικοί* (Italikoi) and *Ἰταλιώτης* (*Italiotes*) to refer to the peoples and inhabitants of Italy.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] The adjective *Italianus* emerged in the [medieval period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Latin) and was used as an alternative alongside *Italicus* into the [early modern period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_modern_period).[^cite_note-102]

The [Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy_\(Ostrogothic\)) was created after the [fall of the Western Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_the_Western_Roman_Empire). The name "Italia" was retained for the [kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Lombards) under the [Lombards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombards) and later their [successor kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy_\(Holy_Roman_Empire\)) within the [Holy Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire).[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104]

## History

Main article: [Population history of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_history_of_Italy)

Further information: [History of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Italy)

### Roman era

Further information: [Roman Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Italy), [Ancient peoples of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_peoples_of_Italy), [List of ancient Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ancient_Italic_peoples), [Etruscan civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscan_civilization), [Roman people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_people), [Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_peoples), [Magna Graecia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magna_Graecia), [Cisalpine Gaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisalpine_Gaul), and [Ancient Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome)



The [Capitoline Wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitoline_Wolf) ([Italian](14708.md): *Lupa Capitolina*) is a [bronze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze) sculpture depicting a scene from the legend of the [founding of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_of_Rome). The sculpture shows a [she-wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/She-wolf_\(Roman_mythology\)) suckling the mythical twin founders of Rome, [Romulus and Remus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romulus_and_Remus). The image of the she-wolf suckling Romulus and Remus is a symbol of Rome since ancient times, and one of the most recognizable icons of ancient mythology.

[^cite_note-105]

The [Italian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_peninsula) was divided into multiple tribal or ethnic territories prior to the [Roman conquest of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_expansion_in_Italy) in the 3rd century BCE. The [Latins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latins_\(Italic_tribe\)), with [Rome](25458.md) as their capital, came to dominate the Italian peninsula by 218 BCE. They continued to expand beyond Italy, and after a [century-long struggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punic_Wars) against [Carthage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carthage), Rome conquered Sicily, Sardinia, and Corsica. By the end of the [Third Punic War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Punic_War) in 146 BCE, Rome had completely destroyed Carthage and become the dominant power in the Mediterranean. Unification and [Romanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_\(cultural\)) of Italy culminated in 88 BC, when, in the aftermath of the [Social War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_War_\(91%E2%80%9388_BC\)), Rome granted [Roman citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_citizenship) to all fellow [Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_peoples).[^cite_note-106]

Rome was originally a republican city-state, but four famous civil conflicts destroyed the [Roman Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Republic): [Sulla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucius_Cornelius_Sulla) against [Marius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaius_Marius) (88–82 BCE), [Julius Caesar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Caesar) against [Pompey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pompey) (49–45 BC), [Brutus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Junius_Brutus) and [Cassius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaius_Cassius_Longinus) against [Mark Antony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Antony) and [Octavian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octavian) (43 BC), and Mark Antony against Octavian. Octavian, the final victor (31 BC), became the first Roman Emperor.

During the [Crisis of the Third Century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_of_the_Third_Century), the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) nearly collapsed under the combined pressures of invasions, civil wars, and hyperinflation. In 284 CE, Emperor [Diocletian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocletian) restored political stability. He divided the Roman Empire's territory and [administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetrarchy) into the [Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Roman_Empire) and [Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire) Empires. [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_church_of_the_Roman_Empire) became the Roman state religion in AD 380, under Emperor [Theodosius I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodosius_I). The defeat of the last Western Roman emperor, [Romulus Augustulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romulus_Augustulus), by Germanic general [Odoacer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odoacer) marked the end of the Western Roman Empire (and political unification of Italy until the establishment of the modern [Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy) in 1861).

### The Middle Ages

Further information: [Italy in the Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy_in_the_Middle_Ages)



[Odoacer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odoacer), the first [King of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Italy)

[Odoacer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odoacer) ruled as the first king of Italy. After the death of his successor [Theodoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodoric_the_Great) in 526 CE, the kingdom began to grow weak. By 553 CE, Byzantine Emperor [Justinian I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justinian_I) expelled the Ostrogoths from Italy and brought it back under [Roman control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire_under_the_Justinian_dynasty). However, within twenty years, the [Lombards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombards) invaded Italy and [conquered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Lombards) most of the peninsula. (Remnants of Byzantine control remained in [Southern Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Italy) until the [Arab conquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Islam_in_southern_Italy) of [Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirate_of_Sicily) in the 9th century and the [Norman conquest of Southern Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_conquest_of_Southern_Italy) in the 11th; the interaction among Latin, Byzantine, Arab, and Norman cultures resulted in the formation of a unique [Norman-Arab-Byzantine culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman-Arab-Byzantine_culture) in Southern Italy.)



[Marco Polo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Polo), explorer of the 13th century, recorded his 24 years-long travels in the *[Book of the Marvels of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Travels_of_Marco_Polo)*, introducing Europeans to Central Asia and China.

[^cite_note-Marco_Polo_–_Exploration-107]

For two centuries following the Lombard invasion, the [popes](23056.md) opposed foreign rule in Italy. They ultimately defeated the Lombards, with the aid of two Frankish kings, [Pepin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepin_the_Short) and [Charlemagne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlemagne), and established the [Papal States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_States) in central Italy in 756. To cement the Church's alliance with Charlemagne, [Pope Leo III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Leo_III) crowned him the Roman Emperor in 800.[^cite_note-108] Members of the [Carolingian dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolingian_dynasty) continued to rule Italy, and this [Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy_\(Holy_Roman_Empire\)) became part of the [Holy Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire) in the 10th century.

### The Renaissance and the Age of Discovery

Further information: [Italian city-states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_city-states) and [Italian language § Origins](14708.md#Origins)



[Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci), a [polymath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymath) of the [High Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Renaissance) who was active as a painter, [draughtsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drawing), engineer, scientist, theorist, sculptor, and architect

[^cite_note-109]

From the 11th century on, Italian cities rapidly grew in independence and importance, becoming centers of political life, [banking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking), and foreign trade. Many, including [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence), [Rome](25458.md), [Genoa](47332321.md), [Milan](36511.md), [Pisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisa), [Siena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siena) and [Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice), grew into nearly independent city-states and [maritime republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_republics), each with its own foreign policy and trade. By the 14th and 15th centuries, some Italian city-states, such as Venice and Florence, ranked among the most influential powers in Europe. The Italian merchant cities acted as a gateway for goods and ideas from the Byzantine and [Islamic](22278.md) world into Europe; the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance) began in Florence in the 14th century[^cite_note-Burke1998-110] and led to an unparalleled flourishing of the arts, literature, music, and science.



The Italian explorer [Christopher Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus) leads an expedition to the New World, 1492. [His voyages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voyages_of_Christopher_Columbus) are celebrated as the discovery of the Americas from a European perspective, and they opened a [new era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_modern_period) in the history of humankind and sustained contact between the two worlds.



[Amerigo Vespucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amerigo_Vespucci), Italian explorer from whose name the term "[America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naming_of_the_Americas)" is derived

[^cite_note-livescience.com-111]

Italian[^cite_note-112] [explorers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_explorers) and navigators, eager to find alternative trade routes to the Indies, ushered in the [Age of Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Discovery) and the European colonization of the Americas. Notable among them were: [Christopher Columbus (Cristoforo Colombo)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus), who discovered the New World and opened the Americas for European conquest;[^cite_note-113] [John Cabot (Giovanni Caboto)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Cabot), the first European to arrive in [Newfoundland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newfoundland);[^cite_note-114] [Amerigo Vespucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amerigo_Vespucci) (for whom the [Americas were named](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naming_of_the_Americas)), who demonstrated circa 1501 that the New World was not Asia but a previously unknown continent;[^cite_note-115] and [Giovanni da Verrazzano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_da_Verrazzano), the first European to explore the Atlantic coast of [North America](21139.md).[^cite_note-116]

### The French Revolution and Napoleon

Main article: [Napoleonic Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Kingdom_of_Italy)



[Laura Bassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Bassi), the first chairwoman of a university in a scientific field of studies



[Giuseppe Compagnoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Compagnoni), considered the "father of the [flag of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Italy)"

The [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution) began in 1789 and immediately found supporters among the Italian people. After the [French king](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_XVI) was overthrown and France became a [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_First_Republic), secret clubs favouring an Italian republic were formed throughout Italy. In 1796, [Napoleon Bonaparte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte) led a French army into northern Italy and drove out [its Austrian rulers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Habsburg). Napoleon made himself emperor in 1804; parts of northern and central Italy were unified under the name of the Kingdom of Italy, with Napoleon as king, the rest was annexed by France. French domination, which lasted less than 20 years, brought representative assemblies and new laws that were uniform across the country; for the first time since ancient Rome, Italians from different regions were using the same money and served in the same army. Many Italians began to see the possibility of a united Italy free of foreign control.

### Italian unification and the Kingdom of Italy

Main articles: [Italian unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_unification), [Proclamation of the Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_the_Kingdom_of_Italy), and [Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy)

Goffredo Mameli

Michele Novaro

On the left, [Goffredo Mameli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goffredo_Mameli), author of the lyrics; on the right, [Michele Novaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michele_Novaro), composer of the music, of the song *[Il Canto degli Italiani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Il_Canto_degli_Italiani)*, the Italian [national anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_anthem) since 1946

In the aftermath of [Napoleon's defeat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Waterloo) and the [Congress of Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna), Italy came under control of the [Austrian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_Empire) and the [Habsburg dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg_dynasty). [Italian nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_nationalism) movements, led by reformers such as [Giuseppe Mazzini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Mazzini), occurred in several parts of the peninsula from the 1830s to 1849. The [Risorgimento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Risorgimento) revolution of the 1850s was ultimately successful, and on 17 March 1861, [Victor Emmanuel II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_II) was proclaimed king of the [Kingdom of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_the_Kingdom_of_Italy).





[Victor Emmanuel II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_II_of_Italy) (*left*) and [Camillo Benso, Count of Cavour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camillo_Benso,_Count_of_Cavour) (*right*), leading figures in the [Italian unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_unification), became respectively the first king and first Prime Minister of unified Italy.

[Italian troops occupied Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capture_of_Rome) in 1870, and in July 1871, it formally became the capital of the kingdom. [Pope Pius IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX), a longtime rival of Italian kings, stated he had been made a "prisoner" inside the [Vatican](32408.md) walls and refused to cooperate with the royal administration. Only in 1929 did the pope accept the unified Italy with [Rome](25458.md) as its capital.

The process of Italian unification was completed in [World War I](4764461.md), with the annexation of Trieste, [Istria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istria), [Trentino-Alto Adige](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trentino-Alto_Adige), and [Zara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zadar). After World War I, Italy emerged as one of the [world's four great powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Four_\(World_War_I\)). In the decades following unification, Italy began creating colonies in [Africa](5334607.md), and under [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini)'s [Fascist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist_Italy_\(1922%E2%80%931943\)) conquered [Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Empire), founding the [Italian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Empire) in 1936. The population of Italy grew to 45 million in 1940 and the economy, which had been based upon agriculture until that time, started its industrial development, mainly in northern Italy.

### The Italian Republic

Main article: [History of the Italian Republic](1161302.md)



[Alcide De Gasperi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcide_De_Gasperi), [first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_Italy) republican [Prime Minister of Italy](273741.md) and one of the [Founding Fathers of the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_European_Union)

On 2 June 1946, Italy held its first free election after more than 20 years of Fascist rule. Italians [chose to replace the monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Italian_institutional_referendum), which had been closely associated with [Fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism), with a republic. They elected a [Constituent Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constituent_Assembly_of_Italy) of anti-fascist representatives, which created a new democratic [Italian constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_constitution) in 1947.[^cite_note-auto2-117]



A young Italian exile on the run carries, along with her personal effects, a [flag of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Italy), during the [Istrian-Dalmatian exodus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istrian-Dalmatian_exodus).

Under the [Treaty of Peace with Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Peace_with_Italy,_1947), [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia) annexed [Istria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istria), [Kvarner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kvarner_Gulf), most of the [Julian March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_March), and [Zara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zadar), which led to the [emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istrian-Dalmatian_exodus) of between 230,000 and 350,000 ethnic Italians, Slovenians, Croatians, and [Istro-Romanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istro-Romanians), who chose to maintain [Italian citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_citizenship).[^cite_note-118]

In 1949 Italy became a member of [NATO](21133.md). The [Marshall Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Plan) helped to revive the Italian economy which, until the late 1960s, enjoyed a period of sustained economic growth commonly called the "[Economic Miracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_economic_miracle)". In 1957, Italy was a founding member of the [European Economic Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Economic_Community) (EEC), which became the [European Union](9317.md) (EU) in 1993.

## Ethnogenesis

Further information: [Genetic history of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_history_of_Europe), [Genetic history of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_history_of_Italy), [Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_peoples), and [List of ancient peoples of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ancient_peoples_of_Italy)



[Principal component analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principal_component_analysis) of the Italian population with other populations

Due to historic demographic shifts in the [Italian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_peninsula) throughout history, its geographical position in the center of the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), and Italy's regional ethnic diversity since ancient times, modern Italians are genetically diverse.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120]

### Bronze Age

Further information: [Prehistoric Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric_Italy) and [Indo-European migrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_migrations)

Italians, like most Europeans, largely descend from three distinct lineages:[^cite_note-Indo-European-121] Paleolithic [hunter-gatherers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer), such as the [Epigravettian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epigravettian) culture, who arrived in the Italian peninsula as early as 35,000 to 40,000 years ago;[^cite_note-122] Neolithic [Early European Farmers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_European_Farmers) who migrated from [Western Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Asia) and the [Middle East](19323.md) during the [Neolithic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic_Revolution) 9,000 years ago;[^cite_note-123] and [Yamnaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamnaya_culture) [Steppe pastoralists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Steppe_Herders) who expanded into Europe from the [Pontic–Caspian steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontic%E2%80%93Caspian_steppe) of Ukraine and southern Russia during the Indo-European migrations 5,000 years ago.[^cite_note-Indo-European-121]

The first wave of [Indo-European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_languages) migrations into Italy in the [Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze_Age) occurred from [Central Europe](5188.md) (e.g. the [Bell Beaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Beaker) culture), followed by the [Italo-Celts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italo-Celtic) (e.g. the Celtic-speaking [Canegrate culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canegrate_culture)[^cite_note-124] and the Italic-speaking [Proto-Villanovan culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Villanovan_culture),[^cite_note-Menotti2004-125] both deriving from the Proto-Italo-Celtic [Urnfield culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urnfield_culture)). Recent DNA studies confirmed the arrival of [Steppe-related ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Steppe_Herders) in Northern Italy to at least 2000 BCE and in Central Italy by 1600 BCE, with this ancestry component increasing through time.[^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128] In the late Bronze Age and early [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age), Celtic-speaking [La Tène](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_T%C3%A8ne_culture) and [Hallstatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallstatt_culture) cultures spread over a large part of Italy,[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131] with related archeological artifacts found as far south as [Apulia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apulia).[^cite_note-Prichard1826-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-Hazlitt1851-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137] [Italics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_peoples) occupied northeastern, southern, and central Italy. The "West Italic" group (including the [Latins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latins_\(Italic_tribe\))) were the first wave. Major tribes included the Latins and [Falisci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falisci) in Lazio; the [Oenotrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oenotrians) and Italii in [Calabria](44772.md); the [Ausones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausones), [Aurunci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurunci) and [Opici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opici) in [Campania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campania); and perhaps the [Veneti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic_Veneti) in [Veneto](43780.md) and the [Sicels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicels) in Sicily. They were followed, and largely displaced by the East Italic ([Osco-Umbrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osco-Umbrian_languages)) group.[^cite_note-138]

### Iron Age



Ethnolinguistic map of Italy in the [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age), before the [Roman expansion in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_expansion_in_Italy)

During the Iron Age, prior to Roman rule, Italy was predominantly inhabited by [Italic tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_tribes). The peoples living in the area of modern Italy and the islands were:

- **[Etruscans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscans)** ([Camunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camunni), [Lepontii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lepontii), [Raeti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raeti));

- **[Sicani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicani)** in [Sicily](27619.md)

- **[Elymians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elymians)**;

- **[Ligures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligures)** ([Apuani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apuani), [Bagienni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagienni), [Briniates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Briniates), [Corsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corsi_people), [Friniates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friniates), [Garuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garuli), [Hercates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hercates), [Ilvates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilvates), [Insubres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insubres), [Orobii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orobii), [Laevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laevi), [Lapicini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapicini), [Marici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marici_\(Ligures\)), [Statielli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statielli), [Taurini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taurini));

- **[Italics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_tribes)** ([Latins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latins_\(Italic_tribe\)), [Falisci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falisci), [Marsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsi), [Umbri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbri), [Volsci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volsci), [Marrucini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marrucini), [Osci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osci), [Aurunci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurunci), [Ausones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausones), [Campanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campanians), [Paeligni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paeligni), [Sabines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabines), [Bruttii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruttii), [Frentani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frentani), [Lucani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucanians), [Samnites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samnites), [Pentri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentri), [Caraceni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caraceni_\(tribe\)), [Caudini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caudini), [Hirpini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirpini), [Aequi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aequi), [Fidenates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidenae), [Hernici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernici), [Picentes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picentes), [Vestini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vestini), [Morgeti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgantina), [Sicels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicels), [Veneti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic_Veneti));

- **[Iapygians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iapygians)** ([Messapians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messapians), [Daunians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daunians), [Peucetians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peucetians));

- **[Celts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisalpine_Gaul)** ([Allobroges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allobroges), [Ausones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausones), [Boii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boii_\(Cisalpine_Gaul\)), [Carni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carni), [Cenomani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cenomani_\(Cisalpine_Gaul\)), [Ceutrones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceutrones_\(Alps\)), [Graioceli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graioceli), [Lepontii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lepontii), [Lingones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingones), [Segusini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segusini), [Senones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senones), [Salassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salassi), [Veragri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veragri), [Vertamocorii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertamocorii_\(Cisalpina\)));

- **[Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magna_Graecia)** of [Magna Graecia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magna_Graecia);

- **[Sardinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sardinians)** ([Nuragic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuragic_civilization) [tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nuragic_tribes)), in [Sardinia](29376.md)

By the beginning of the [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age_Italy), the [Etruscans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscan_civilization) had emerged as the dominant civilization on the Italian peninsula. The Etruscans, expanded from [Etruria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruria) over a large part of Italy, covering what is now [Tuscany](21967242.md), western [Umbria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbria), and northern [Lazio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lazio),[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140] as well as what are now the [Po Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Po_Valley), [Emilia-Romagna](162715.md), south-eastern [Lombardy](43807.md), southern [Veneto](43780.md), and western [Campania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campania).[^cite_note-Camporeale2004-141][^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-belsito-144][^cite_note-145] Ancient authors report [several hypotheses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscan_origins) for the origin of the Etruscans, including that they came from the Aegean Sea. Modern archaeological and genetic research concluded that the Etruscans were [autochthonous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villanovan_culture) and had a genetic profile similar to their Latin neighbors, notably lacking recent admixture with Anatolia or the Eastern Mediterranean.[^cite_note-Ghirotto2013-146][^cite_note-Tassi2013-147][^cite_note-Leonardi2018-148][^cite_note-149][^cite_note-Antonio2019-150][^cite_note-Posth2021-151]



Fresco of dancing [Peucetian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peucetians) women in the [Tomb of the Dancers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomb_of_the_Dancers) in [Ruvo di Puglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruvo_di_Puglia), 4th–5th century BC

The Ligures were one of the oldest populations in Italy and Western Europe,[^cite_note-152] possibly of Pre-Indo-European origin.[^cite_note-153] According to Strabo, they were not Celts but later became influenced by the Celtic culture of their neighbours, and thus are sometimes referred to as Celticized Ligurians or Celto-Ligurians.[^cite_note-SG-154] Their language had similarities to both [Italic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_languages) ([Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) and the [Osco-Umbrian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osco-Umbrian_languages)) and [Celtic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celtic_languages) ([Gaulish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaulish_language)).[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] They primarily inhabited the regions of [Liguria](71864.md), [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont), northern [Tuscany](21967242.md), western [Lombardy](43807.md), western [Emilia-Romagna](162715.md) and northern [Sardinia](29376.md), but are believed to have once occupied an even larger portion of ancient Italy as far south as [Sicily](27619.md).[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] They were also settled in [Corsica](5714828.md) and in the [Provence](48503.md) region along the southern coast of modern [France](5843419.md).

Beginning in the 8th century BCE, Greeks arrived in Italy and founded cities along the coast of southern Italy and eastern Sicily, which became known as [Magna Graecia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magna_Graecia) ("Greater Greece"). The Greeks were frequently at war with the native Italic tribes, but nonetheless managed to [Hellenize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenization) and assimilate much of the indigenous population located along eastern Sicily and the Southern coasts of the Italian mainland.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] According to [Beloch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Julius_Beloch), the number of Greek citizens in southern Italy reached only 80,000 to 90,000 at most, while the local people subjected by the Greeks were between 400,000 and 600,000.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-LP-163] By the 4th and 3rd centuries BCE, Greek power in Italy was challenged and began to decline, and many Greeks were pushed out of peninsular Italy by the native [Oscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscans), [Brutti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brutti), and [Lucani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucanians) tribes.[^cite_note-164]



A mounted Lucanian warrior, fresco from a tomb of [Paestum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paestum), Italy, c. 360 BC

The [Gauls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gauls) crossed the Alps and [invaded northern Italy in the 4th and 3rd centuries BC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman-Gallic_wars#Cisalpine), settling in the area that became known as [Cisalpine Gaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisalpine_Gaul). Although named after the Gauls, the region was mostly inhabited by indigenous tribes, namely the Ligures, Etruscans, [Euganei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euganei), and Veneti. Estimates by Beloch and [Brunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Brunt) suggest that in the 3rd century BCE, Gaulish settlers in north Italy numbered between 130,000 and 140,000 out of a total population of about 1.4 million.[^cite_note-LP-163][^cite_note-Ligt,_2012._p._43-44-165] The northern half of Cisalpine Gaul was already inhabited by the Celtic [Lepontii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lepontii) since the Bronze Age. Speaking about the Alpine region, the Greek historian [Strabo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strabo), wrote:

> *The [Alps](981.md) are inhabited by numerous nations, but all [Keltic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celts) with the exception of the Ligurians, and these, though of a different race, closely resemble them in their manner of life.*[^cite_note-SG-154]

According to [Pliny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pliny_the_Elder) and [Livy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livy), after the invasion of the Gauls, some of the Etruscans living in the Po Valley sought refuge in the Alps and became known as the [Raeti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raeti).[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] The Raeti inhabited the region of [Trentino-Alto Adige](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trentino-Alto_Adige), as well as eastern [Switzerland](26748.md) and [Tyrol](148994.md) in western [Austria](26964606.md). The [Ladins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ladin_people) of north-eastern Italy and the [Romansh people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romansh_people) of Switzerland are said to be descended from the Raeti.[^cite_note-168]

### Roman

Main article: [Colonia (Roman)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(Roman\))



Map of Roman coloniae during the second century in Italy

&#x20;The [Romans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome) — who according to legend originally consisted of [three ancient tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_tribe): Latins, Sabines and Etruscans[^cite_note-169] — would go on to [conquer the whole Italian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_conquest_of_Italy). During the Roman period hundreds of cities and colonies were established throughout Italy, including [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence), [Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turin), [Como](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Como), [Pavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavia), [Padua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padua), [Verona](263991.md), [Vicenza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicenza), [Trieste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste) and many others. After the Roman conquest of Italy, "the whole of Italy had become Latinized".[^cite_note-170] The Romans continued expansion northward to [conquer Cisalpine Gaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisalpine_Gaul#Gallic_expansion_and_Roman_conquest) and establish colonies in the former Gallic territory, including [Bologna](21069333.md), [Modena](62418.md), [Reggio Emilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reggio_Emilia), [Parma](24231.md), [Piacenza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piacenza), [Cremona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cremona) and [Forlì](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forl%C3%AC).[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-Strabo,_5.1.10-172] According to [Strabo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strabo):

> *The Cispadane peoples occupy all that country which is encircled by the Apennine Mountains towards the Alps as far as Genua and Sabata. The greater part of the country used to be occupied by the Boii, Ligures, Senones, and [Gaesatae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaesatae); but since the Boii have been driven out, and since both the Gaesatae and the Senones have been annihilated, only the Ligurian tribes and the Roman colonies are left.*[^cite_note-Strabo,_5.1.10-172]

The [Boii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boii_\(Cisalpine_Gaul\)), the most powerful and numerous of the Gallic tribes, were expelled by the Romans after 191 BCE and settled in [Bohemia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemia), while the [Insubres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insubres) still lived in [Mediolanum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediolanum) in the 1st century BCE.[^cite_note-173]

Latin colonies were founded at [Ariminum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimini) in 268 BCE and at [Firmum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fermo) in 264 BCE,[^cite_note-174] while large numbers of [Picentes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Picentes), who previously inhabited the region, were moved to [Paestum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paestum) and settled along the river [Silarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sele_\(river\)) in [Campania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campania). Between 180 and 179 BCE, 47,000 Ligures belonging to the Apuani tribe were removed from their home along the modern Ligurian-Tuscan border and deported to [Samnium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samnium), an area corresponding to inland Campania, while Latin colonies were established in their place at [Pisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisa), [Lucca](46169.md) and [Luni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luni,_Italy).[^cite_note-175] Such population movements contributed to the rapid Romanization and Latinization of [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Italy).[^cite_note-176]

During the Imperial period, the dominant genetic trend for Rome and Central Italy was a notable shift towards Eastern Mediterranean ancestry, with an increase in a Neolithic Iranian component of individuals in samples found in these areas compared to those from the Bronze Age. Studies have found that there was considerably more genetic influence from the Eastern Mediterranean than other parts of the Roman empire found in the Imperial Roman samples [^cite_note-177].

### Middle Ages



[Lombard (Northern Italian) colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombards_of_Sicily) of Sicily: in light blue: the cities where Gallo-Italic language is spoken today. In dark blue: the cities where there is a good influence of the Gallo-Italic language. In purple: ancient Gallo-Italic colonies, the influence in these cities is variable, also some districts of [Messina](59705.md) were colonized.

A large Germanic confederation of [Sciri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciri), [Heruli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heruli), [Turcilingi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turcilingi), and [Rugians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugians), led by [Odoacer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odoacer), invaded and settled Italy in 476 CE.[^cite_note-178] They were preceded by the [Alemanni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alemanni), including 30,000 warriors with their families, who settled in the Po Valley in 371 CE,[^cite_note-179] and by [Burgundians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgundians) who settled between Northwestern Italy and Southern France in 443 CE.[^cite_note-Vauthier_Adams-180] The Germanic tribe of the [Ostrogoths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostrogoths) led by [Theodoric the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodoric_the_Great) conquered Italy and, in order to legitimize their rule to Roman subjects who believed in the superiority of Roman culture over foreign "[barbarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbarian)" culture, created a blended [Romano-Germanic culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romano-Germanic_culture).[^cite_note-181] Since Italy had a population of several million, the Goths did not constitute a significant addition to the local population: at most, several thousand Ostrogoths in a population of 6 or 7 million.[^cite_note-Vauthier_Adams-180][^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] After the [Gothic War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_War_\(535%E2%80%93554\)), which devastated the local population, the Ostrogoths were defeated. Nevertheless, according to Roman historian [Procopius of Caesarea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procopius_of_Caesarea), the Ostrogothic population was allowed to live peacefully in Italy with their Rugian allies under Roman sovereignty.[^cite_note-184]

In the sixth century, another Germanic tribe known as the [Longobards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombards) invaded Italy, which in the meantime had been reconquered by the East Roman or Byzantine Empire. The Longobards were a small minority compared to the roughly 4 million inhabitants of Italy at the time.[^cite_note-Santosuosso_44-185] They were later followed by the [Bavarians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bavarian_dynasty) and the [Franks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franks), who conquered and ruled most of Italy. Some groups of [Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavs) settled in parts of the northern Italian Peninsula between the 7th and 8th centuries,[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188] while [Bulgars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgars) led by [Alcek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcek) settled in [Sepino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepino), [Bojano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojano), and [Isernia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isernia). These Bulgars preserved their speech and identity until the late 8th century.[^cite_note-189]



Map of Tuscan settlements in Sicily.

Following Roman rule, Sicily, Corsica, and Sardinia were conquered by the [Vandals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vandals), then by the Ostrogoths, and finally by the Byzantines. Sicily was later invaded by the [Arabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Islam_in_southern_Italy) in the 9th century and the [Normans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_conquest_of_Southern_Italy) in the 11th century, leading to the formation of a unique [Norman-Arab-Byzantine culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman-Arab-Byzantine_culture) in Sicily. During the subsequent [Swabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hohenstaufen) rule under the Holy Roman Emperor [Frederick II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor), who spent most of his life as [king of Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sicily) in his court in [Palermo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palermo), Moors were progressively eradicated until the massive deportation of the last [Muslims](19541.md) of Sicily.[^cite_note-190] As a result of the Arab expulsion, many towns across Sicily were left depopulated. By the 12th century, [Swabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hohenstaufen) kings granted immigrants from northern Italy (particularly [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont), [Lombardy](43807.md) and [Liguria](71864.md)), [Latium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latium) and [Tuscany](21967242.md) in central Italy, and [French regions](5843419.md) of [Normandy](21724.md), [Provence](48503.md) and [Brittany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brittany) (all collectively known as *[Lombards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lombards_of_Sicily)*.)[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192] settlement into Sicily, re-establishing the Latin element into the island, a legacy which can be seen in the many [Gallo-Italic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallo-Italic_of_Sicily) dialects and towns found in the interior and western parts of Sicily, brought by these settlers.[^cite_note-193] Before them, other *Lombards* arrived in [Sicily](27619.md), with an expedition departed in 1038, led by the Byzantine commander [George Maniakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Maniakes),[^cite_note-194] which for a very short time managed to snatch Messina and [Syracuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syracuse,_Sicily) from [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab) rule. The Lombards who arrived with the Byzantines settled in [Maniace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maniace), [Randazzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randazzo) and [Troina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troina), while a group of [Genoese](47332321.md) and other *Lombards* from Liguria settled in [Caltagirone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caltagirone).[^cite_note-195] After the marriage between the Norman [Roger I of Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_I_of_Sicily) and [Adelaide del Vasto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adelaide_del_Vasto), descendant of the [Aleramici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleramici) family, many Northern Italian colonisers (known collectively as *Lombards*) left their homeland, in the Aleramici's possessions in [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont) and [Liguria](71864.md) (then known as *Lombardy*), to settle on the island of Sicily.[^cite_note-books.google.it-196][^cite_note-197] It is believed that the Lombard immigrants in Sicily over a couple of centuries were a total of about 200,000.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] An estimated 20,000 [Swabians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swabians) and 40,000 [Normans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normans) settled in the southern half of Italy during the 10th and 11th centuries.[^cite_note-201] Additional Tuscan migrants settled in Sicily after the Florentine conquest of Pisa in 1406.[^cite_note-202]

Some of the Muslims expelled by the Normans were deported to [Lucera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucera) (Lugêrah, as it was known in Arabic). Their numbers eventually reached between 15,000 and 20,000,[^cite_note-203] leading Lucera to be called *Lucaera Saracenorum* because it represented the last stronghold of Islamic presence in Italy. The colony thrived for 75 years until it was sacked in 1300 by Christian forces under the command of the [Angevin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capetian_House_of_Anjou) [Charles II of Naples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_II_of_Naples). The city's Muslim inhabitants were exiled or sold into slavery.[^cite_note-204] After the expulsions of Muslims in Lucera, Charles II replaced Lucera's Saracens with Christians, chiefly [Burgundian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgundy_\(region\)) and [Provençal](48503.md) soldiers and farmers,[^cite_note-205] following an initial settlement of 140 Provençal families in 1273.[^cite_note-206] A remnant of the descendants of these Provençal colonists, still speaking a [Franco-Provençal dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco-Proven%C3%A7al_language), has survived until the present day in the villages of [Faeto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faeto) and [Celle di San Vito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celle_di_San_Vito).

### Modern period





[Giuseppe Mazzini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Mazzini) *(left)*, highly influential leader of the Italian revolutionary movement; and [Giuseppe Garibaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Garibaldi) *(right)*, celebrated as one of the greatest generals of modern times

[^cite_note-scholar_and_patriot-207]

&#x20;and as the "Hero of the Two Worlds",

[^cite_note-Garibaldi_on_Encyclopædia_Britannica-208]

&#x20;who commanded and fought in many military campaigns that led to [Italian unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_unification)

Substantial migrations of Lombards to Naples, Rome, and Palermo continued in the 16th and 17th centuries, driven by the constant overcrowding in the north.[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] Minor but significant settlements of [Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavs) and [Arbereshe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arb%C3%ABresh%C3%AB_people) in Italy have been recorded, while [Scottish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_people) soldiers – the *[Garde Ecossaise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Guards_\(France\))* – who served the French King, [Francis I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_I_of_France), settled in the mountains of [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont).[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212]

The geographical and cultural proximity with Southern Italy pushed Albanians to cross the [Strait of Otranto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait_of_Otranto), especially after [Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg)'s death and the conquest of the [Balkans](4829.md) by the [Ottomans](22278.md). In defense of the Christian religion and in search of soldiers loyal to the Spanish crown, [Alfonso V of Aragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfonso_V_of_Aragon), also king of Naples, invited Arbereshe soldiers to move to Italy with their families. In return the king guaranteed to Albanians lots of land and a favourable taxation.

Arbereshe and Schiavoni were used to repopulate abandoned villages or villages whose population had died in earthquakes, plagues and other catastrophes. Albanian soldiers were also used to quell rebellions in Calabria. Slavic colonies were established in eastern [Friuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friuli),[^cite_note-213] [Sicily](27619.md)[^cite_note-214] and [Molise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molise) ([Molise Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molise_Croats)).[^cite_note-215]

Between the [Late Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Middle_Ages) and the [early modern period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_modern_period), there were several waves of immigration of Albanians into Italy, in addition to another in the 20th century.[^cite_note-216] The descendants of these Albanian emigrants, many still retaining the [Albanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language), the [Arbëresh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arb%C3%ABresh_language) dialect, have survived throughout southern Italy, numbering about 260,000 people,[^cite_note-RhzMMTTdIC_2010,_p._18-217] with roughly 80,000 to 100,000 speaking the Albanian language.[^cite_note-books.google.bg-218][^cite_note-ethnologue.com-219]

### Italian Surnames

Most of [Italy's surnames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_name#Surnames) (*cognomi*), with the exception of a few areas marked by linguistic minorities, derive from Italian. Many are derived from an individual's physical qualities (e.g. *Rossi*, *Bianchi*, *Quattrocchi*, *Mancini*, *Grasso*, etc.), occupation (*Ferrari*, *Auditore*, *Sartori*, *Tagliabue*, etc.), fatherhood or lack thereof (*De Pretis*, *Orfanelli*, *Esposito*, *Trovato*, etc.), and geographic location (*[Padovano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padua)*, *[Pisano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisa)*, *[Leccese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lecce)*, *[Lucchese](46169.md)*, etc.). Some of them also indicate a remote foreign origin (*[Greco](42056.md)*, *[Tedesco](152735.md)*, *[Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moors)*, *[Albanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians)*, etc.).
