[Italian migration outside Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_diaspora) occurred over centuries in a series of migration cycles.[^cite_note-Cohen-221] A large [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_diaspora) took place after Italy's unification in 1861 and continued through 1914 with the lead up to the [First World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_World_War).[^cite_note-gabaccia-222][^cite_note-SN-223] One major motivation for emigrants at this time was a post-unification economic slump within Italy (except for the "industrial triangle" between [Milan](36511.md), [Genoa](47332321.md) and [Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turin))[^cite_note-gabaccia-222] that coincided with a boom in industrialization, urbanization, and economic expansion elsewhere in the world, which provided better economic opportunities.[^cite_note-SN-223] Large-scale emigration continued through the late 1920s, well into the Fascist regime, and a subsequent wave was observed after the end of the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War). Another wave of migration outside Italy began in the 21st century and is still ongoing, caused by the [debt crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_government_debt) in Italy.



[Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon), the most notable [Italian French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_France) personality. He was ethnically Italian of Corsican origin, and his family was of [Genoese](47332321.md) and [Tuscan](21967242.md) ancestry.

[^cite_note-224]



[Pope Francis](1687680.md), an [Argentine of Italian descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentines).

[^cite_note-225]

&#x20;About 60% of Argentina's population has Italian ancestry.

[^cite_note-226]

Over 80 million people claiming full or partial Italian descent live outside Italy. A majority of these, about 50 million total, live in [South America](26769.md). [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Brazilian) has the largest number of Italian descendants outside Italy,[^cite_note-Brazil-72] and in [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentine), over 62.5% of the country's population has at least one Italian ancestor.[^cite_note-Matanza-6] Another 23 million Italian descendants live in North America ([United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_American) and [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Canadian)), 7 to 8 million in other parts of Europe (primarily in [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_France), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_Germany), [Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_Switzerland), and [the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_the_United_Kingdom)), and another 1 million in Oceania ([Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Australian) and [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_New_Zealanders)). To a lesser extent, people of full or partial Italian descent are also found in Africa (particularly in the former Italian colonies of [Eritrea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Eritreans),[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-camera.it-228][^cite_note-babelfish.yahoo.com-229] [Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Somalis), [Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Libyans), and [Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_of_Ethiopia); and in others countries such as [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_South_Africans),[^cite_note-Italian-World-5] [Tunisia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Tunisians), and [Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Egyptians)), in the Middle East (such as the [United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) with 10,000 Italian immigrants), and in Asia ([Singapore](27318.md) is home to a sizeable Italian community).[^cite_note-Italian-World-5]



World map of first level subdivisions (states, counties, provinces, etc.) that are home to [Little Italys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Italy) or Italian neighbourhoods

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Italy)



The [Pantheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantheon,_Rome) and the [Fontana del Pantheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fontana_del_Pantheon). Roman relics and Roman culture are important symbols in Italy.

Further information: [National symbols of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Italy), [Traditions of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditions_of_Italy), and [Folklore of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_of_Italy)

[Italy](14532.md) is considered one of the birthplaces of [Western civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture)[^cite_note-230] and has been described as a [cultural superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_\(international_relations\)#Power_as_status).[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-Washington_Post-232][^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235] Italian culture is incredibly diverse, spanning the entire [Italian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_peninsula) plus [Sardinia](29376.md) and [Sicily](27619.md). Italy was the origin of phenomena of international impact including the [Roman Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Republic), [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome), the [Roman Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church), the [Maritime republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_republics), [Romanesque art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_art), [Scholasticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scholasticism), the [Rinascimento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance), the [Age of Discovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Discovery), [Mannerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannerism), the [Scientific Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_Revolution),[^cite_note-236] the [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque), [Neoclassicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassicism), [Fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism),[^cite_note-237] and [European integration](683704.md).

Italy became a seat of learning in 1088 with the establishment of the [University of Bologna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bologna), the first [university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University) and the [oldest in continuous operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_universities_in_continuous_operation).[^cite_note-Top_Universities-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-Hunt_Janin_2008-240][^cite_note-Ridder-Symoens_1992-241] The [Schola Medica Salernitana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schola_Medica_Salernitana), in southern Italy, the first medical school in Europe, and many other centers of higher education followed.[^cite_note-242] The European [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance) began in Italy and was powered by leading Italian painters, sculptors, architects, scientists, literature authors, and music composers. Italy continued to influence European cultural throughout the [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque) period and into the Romantic period, with a strong Italian presence in music.



[Bologna University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna_University), established in AD 1088, is the [world's oldest university in continuous operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_universities_in_continuous_operation).



The [Victor Emmanuel II Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_II_Monument) in Rome, a [national symbol of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Italy) celebrating the first king of the unified country, and resting place of the [Italian Unknown Soldier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomb_of_the_Unknown_Soldier_\(Italy\)) since the end of World War I. It was inaugurated in 1911, on the occasion of the 50th [Anniversary of the Unification of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anniversary_of_the_Unification_of_Italy).

The country contains several world-famous cities. [Rome](25458.md) was the capital of the ancient Roman Empire, the seat of the Pope of the Catholic Church, and the capital of reunified Italy. [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence) was the heart of the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance).[^cite_note-243] [Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turin), which used to be the capital of Italy, is a center of automobile engineering. [Milan](36511.md) is the industrial and financial capital of Italy and one of the world's [fashion capitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_capital). [Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice)'s intricate canal system attracts tourists from all over the world especially during the [Venetian Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venetian_Carnival) and the [Biennale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biennale). [Naples](55880.md) has the largest historic city center in Europe and the [world's oldest continuously active public opera house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_di_San_Carlo). Due to its relatively late national unification and the historical autonomy of the regions that comprise the Italian peninsula, many Italian traditions and customs can be identified by their regions of origin.

## Philosophy

Main article: [Italian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_philosophy)



[Niccolò Machiavelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niccol%C3%B2_Machiavelli), the founder of modern political science and ethics

[Italian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_literature) had a significant influence on Western philosophy, from the ancient Greeks and Romans, to the Rinascimento, to the [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment), to modern philosophy.

Medieval Italian philosophy was mainly influenced by Christianity. Saint [Thomas Aquinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas) was a [Dominican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Order) theologian, experimentalist, and professor at the [University of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Paris) from the Kingdom of Sicily. Aquinas was notable for introducing a framework of [Aristotelian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristotelianism) to Christian theology.

Major Italian cities like [Rome](25458.md), [Milan](36511.md), [Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice), [Padua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padua), [Bologna](21069333.md) and [Naples](55880.md) – which hosted important universities and an abundance of coffeeshops, which became hubs for intellectual conversation – were centers of scholarship in Enlightenment Europe.[^cite_note-history-world.org-244] Italy was the home of several important philosophers, such as [Giambattista Vico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giambattista_Vico) (who is widely considered the founder of modern Italian philosophy)[^cite_note-maritain.nd.edu-245] and [Antonio Genovesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Genovesi);[^cite_note-history-world.org-244] scientists such as [Alessandro Volta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Volta) and [Luigi Galvani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Galvani);[^cite_note-history-world.org-244] [Cesare Beccaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cesare_Beccaria) (considered one of the fathers of [classical criminal theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_school_\(criminology\)) and modern [penology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penology), who penned one of the earliest prominent condemnations of [torture](47702.md) and th e[death penalty](5902.md)).[^cite_note-history-world.org-244][^cite_note-246]



[Benedetto Croce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedetto_Croce) (*left*) and [Giovanni Gentile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Gentile) (*right*), the two greatest exponents of the [Italian idealism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_idealism)

Some of the most prominent philosophies and ideologies of the late 19th and 20th centuries were developed in Italy, including [anarchism](12.md), [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism), [socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism), [futurism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futurism), [fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism), and [Christian democracy](328969.md). Some notable Italian philosophers in the era include [Antonio Rosmini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Rosmini), the founder of [Italian idealism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_idealism); [Giovanni Gentile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Gentile), an idealist and fascist philosopher; and [Antonio Gramsci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Gramsci), an important philosopher within [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist) and communist theory, credited with creating the theory of [cultural hegemony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_hegemony). [Italian fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fascism) was the official philosophy and ideology of the Italian government from the 1920s to the 1940s led by Benito Mussolini.[^cite_note-Nunzio_Pernicone_pp._111-113-247]

Early [Italian feminists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism_in_Italy) include [Sibilla Aleramo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sibilla_Aleramo), [Alaide Gualberta Beccari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaide_Gualberta_Beccari), and [Anna Maria Mozzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Maria_Mozzoni), although proto-feminist philosophies had previously been explored by earlier Italian writers such as [Christine de Pizan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_de_Pizan), [Moderata Fonte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderata_Fonte), and [Lucrezia Marinella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucrezia_Marinella). Italian physician and educator [Maria Montessori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Montessori) is credited with the creation of the [Montessori philosophy of education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montessori_education).[^cite_note-Montessori-248] [Giuseppe Peano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Peano) was one of the founders of [analytic philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Analytic_philosophy) and contemporary [philosophy of mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophy_of_mathematics). Italian analytic philosophers writing in the 21st century include [Carlo Penco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Penco), [Gloria Origgi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Origgi), [Pieranna Garavaso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pieranna_Garavaso) and [Luciano Floridi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Floridi).[^cite_note-Garin-249]

## Literature

Main article: [Italian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_literature)



[Dante Alighieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dante_Alighieri), whose works helped establish modern [Italian language](14708.md), is considered one of the greatest poets of the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages). His epic poem *[Divine Comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_Comedy)* ranks among the finest works of [world literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_literature).

[^cite_note-250]

Formal Latin literature began in 240 BC, when the first stage play was performed in Rome.[^cite_note-251] The Romans were famous for their poets, dramatists, orators, philosophers, and historians; important figures included [Pliny the Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pliny_the_Elder), [Pliny the Younger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pliny_the_Younger), [Virgil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgil), [Horace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horace), [Propertius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propertius), [Ovid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovid), and [Livy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livy).[^cite_note-252]

Saint [Francis of Assisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi) is widely considered the first Italian poet, with his religious song *[Canticle of the Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canticle_of_the_Sun)*.[^cite_note-253] Notable poets in the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages) include [Dante Alighieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dante_Alighieri), [Petrarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrarch), and [Giovanni Boccaccio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Boccaccio).

During the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance), humanists such as [Leonardo Bruni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Bruni), [Coluccio Salutati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coluccio_Salutati) and [Niccolò Machiavelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niccol%C3%B2_Machiavelli) published important histories and philosophical writings. Philosophers during the [Age of Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment), such as [Apostolo Zeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolo_Zeno) and [Metastasio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metastasio), disseminated their ideas across Europe. [Carlo Goldoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Goldoni), a Venetian playwright and librettist, created the comedy of character. The leading figure of the 18th-century Italian literary revival was [Giuseppe Parini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Parini).



[Alessandro Manzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Manzoni) is famous for the novel *[The Betrothed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Betrothed)* (1827), generally ranked among the masterpieces of world literature.

[^cite_note-254]

&#x20;He contributed to the nationwide use of the Italian language.

[^cite_note-255]

[Giacomo Leopardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Leopardi) was an influential poet in the 19th century, known for his radical views.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] Italian novelists include [Alessandro Manzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Manzoni), author of the historical novel *[I promessi sposi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Betrothed)* ("The Betrothed"); [Italo Svevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italo_Svevo), author of *[La coscienza di Zeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_coscienza_di_Zeno)*; [Luigi Pirandello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Pirandello), winner of the 1934 Nobel Prize in Literature; and [Federigo Tozzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federigo_Tozzi) and [Giuseppe Ungaretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Ungaretti), pioneers of [existentialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Existentialism) in the European novel.

Modern literary figures and Nobel laureates include [Gabriele D'Annunzio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriele_D'Annunzio), nationalist poet [Giosuè Carducci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giosu%C3%A8_Carducci), realist writer [Grazia Deledda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grazia_Deledda), theatre author [Luigi Pirandello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Pirandello), short story writer [Italo Calvino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italo_Calvino), poet [Salvatore Quasimodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvatore_Quasimodo), poet [Eugenio Montale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenio_Montale), [Umberto Eco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umberto_Eco), and satirist and theatre author [Dario Fo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dario_Fo).[^cite_note-258]

## Politics

Main article: [Politics of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Italy)



[Sandro Pertini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Pertini)

The [politics of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Italy) are conducted through a [parliamentary republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic) with a [multi-party system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_system). [Italy](14532.md) has been a [democratic republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_republic) since 2 June 1946, when [the monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy) was abolished by [popular referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Italian_institutional_referendum).[^cite_note-auto2-117] [Executive power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_power) is exercised by the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_\(Italy\)), which is led by the [Prime Minister](273741.md), officially referred to as "President of the Council" (*Presidente del Consiglio*). [Legislative power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_power) is primarily vested in the [two houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) of [Parliament](1417859.md), but the Council of Ministers can introduce bills and holds the majority in both houses. The [judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Italy) is [independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_independence) of the executive and the legislative branches. The [President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Italy) presides over the [High Council of the Judiciary](52834029.md) as the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state), a position that is separate from all three branches.

The Presidents of Italy were [Enrico De Nicola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_De_Nicola), [Luigi Einaudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Einaudi), [Giovanni Gronchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Gronchi), [Antonio Segni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Segni), [Giuseppe Saragat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Saragat), [Giovanni Leone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Leone), [Sandro Pertini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Pertini), [Francesco Cossiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Cossiga), [Oscar Luigi Scalfaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Luigi_Scalfaro), [Carlo Azeglio Ciampi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Azeglio_Ciampi), [Giorgio Napolitano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Napolitano) and [Sergio Mattarella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella).

The legal and social status of Italian women has undergone rapid transformations and changes during the past decades. This includes family laws, the enactment of anti-discrimination measures, and reforms to the penal code (in particular with regard to crimes of violence against women).[^cite_note-europarl.europa.eu-259] After World War II, women were given the right to vote in 1946 Italian institutional referendum. The new Italian Constitution of 1948 affirmed that women had equal rights. It was not however until the 1970s that women in Italy scored some major achievements with the introduction of laws regulating divorce (1970), abortion (1978), and the approval in 1975 of the new family code. Today, women have the same legal rights as men in Italy, and have mainly the same job, business, and education opportunities.[^cite_note-kwin-260]

## Law and justice



[Cesare Beccaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cesare_Beccaria)

Since the Roman Empire, most western contributions to Western legal culture was the emergence of a class of Roman jurists. During the Middle Ages, [Saint Thomas Aquinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas) integrated the theory of natural law with the notion of an eternal and Biblical law.[^cite_note-261] During the Renaissance, Professor [Alberico Gentili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberico_Gentili), the founder of the science of [international law](8195726.md), authored the first treatise on public international law, and separated secular law from canon law and Catholic theology. [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment)'s greatest legal theorists, [Cesare Beccaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cesare_Beccaria), [Giambattista Vico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giambattista_Vico) and [Francesco Mario Pagano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Mario_Pagano), are remembered for their legal works, particularly on criminal law. [Francesco Carrara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Carrara_\(jurist\)), an advocate of abolition of the death penalty, was one of the foremost European criminal lawyers of the 19th century. During the last periods, numerous Italians have been recognised as prominent prosecutor magistrates.

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Italy)



[Enzo Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Ferrari)

The [economy of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Italy) is a [highly developed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country) [social market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_market_economy).[^cite_note-262] It is the third-largest national economy in the [European Union](9317.md), the [10th-largest in the world by nominal GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)), and the [12th-largest by GDP (PPP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)). [Italy](14532.md) is a founding member of the European Union, the [Eurozone](184391.md), the [OECD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development), the [G7](1196634.md) and the [G20](2206608.md);[^cite_note-263] it is the [eighth-largest exporter in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_exports), with \$611 billion exported in 2021. Its closest trade ties are with the other countries of the European Union, with whom it conducts about 59% of its total trade; Italy's [largest trading partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_trading_partners_of_Italy), in order of market share in exports, are [Germany](11867.md) (12.5%), [France](5843419.md) (10.3%), the [United States](3434750.md) (9%), [Spain](26667.md) (5.2%), the [United Kingdom](31717.md) (5.2%) and [Switzerland](26748.md) (4.6%).[^cite_note-264]

In the post-World War II period, Italy saw a transformation from an agriculture-based economy, which had been severely affected by the consequences of the [World Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War), into one of the world's leading countries in [world trade and exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_trade).[^cite_note-265] Italy is the seventh-largest [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing) country,[^cite_note-databank-266] characterised by many [small and medium-sized enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_and_medium-sized_enterprises), with fewer global multinational corporations than other economies of comparable size. Italy is a large manufacturer[^cite_note-267] and exporter[^cite_note-268] of [machinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machinery), [vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicles), [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), furniture, food, clothing, and other products.[^cite_note-269]

Noteworthy Italian entrepreneurs include [Alessandro Martini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Martini), [Luigi Lavazza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Lavazza), [Pietro Ferrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Ferrero), [Giovanni Agnelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Agnelli_\(entrepreneur,_born_1866\)), [Piero Pirelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piero_Pirelli), [Gaspare Campari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaspare_Campari), [Adriano Olivetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriano_Olivetti), [Enzo Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Ferrari), [Ferruccio Lamborghini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferruccio_Lamborghini), [Enrico Mattei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Mattei), [Luciano Benetton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Benetton), and [Giovanni Rana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Rana).

## Visual art

Main article: [Italian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_art)



[Michelangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo)

[Roman art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_art) was influenced by [the art of ancient Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_art), but Roman painting does have unique characteristics. The only surviving Roman paintings are [wall paintings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural), many from villas in [Campania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campania), in Southern Italy. Such paintings can be grouped into four main "styles" or periods[^cite_note-270] and may contain the first examples of [trompe-l'œil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trompe-l'%C5%93il), pseudo-perspective, and pure landscape.[^cite_note-271] Panel painting became more common in Italy during the [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_art) period, under the heavy influence of Byzantine [icons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Icon). [Medieval art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_art) and [Gothic painting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_painting) trended towards realism, with interest in the depiction of volume and perspective, notably by [Cimabue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cimabue) and then his pupil [Giotto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giotto).



[Caravaggio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caravaggio)

The [Italian Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Renaissance) is said by many to be the [golden age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Age_\(metaphor\)) of painting. In Italy, artists like [Paolo Uccello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Uccello), [Fra Angelico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fra_Angelico), [Masaccio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masaccio), [Piero della Francesca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piero_della_Francesca), [Andrea Mantegna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Mantegna), [Filippo Lippi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filippo_Lippi), [Giorgione](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgione), [Tintoretto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tintoretto), [Sandro Botticelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Botticelli), [Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci), [Michelangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo), [Raphael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raphael), [Giovanni Bellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Bellini), and [Titian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titian) developed refined drawing and painting techniques through the use of [perspective](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perspective_\(graphical\)) and the study of [human anatomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_anatomy) and proportion. [Michelangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo) was active as a sculptor from about 1500 to 1520, producing famous works such as his *[David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_\(Michelangelo\))*, *[Pietà](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piet%C3%A0_\(Michelangelo\))*, and *[Moses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moses_\(Michelangelo\)).* Other significant Renaissance sculptors include [Lorenzo Ghiberti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Ghiberti), [Luca Della Robbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_Della_Robbia), [Donatello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donatello), [Filippo Brunelleschi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filippo_Brunelleschi) and [Andrea del Verrocchio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_del_Verrocchio).



[Antonio Canova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Canova)

In the 15th and 16th centuries, the [High Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Renaissance) gave rise to a stylised art known as [mannerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannerism). In place of the balanced compositions and rational approach to perspective that characterised the early Renaissance, the Mannerists sought instability, artifice, and doubt. The unperturbed faces and gestures of [Piero della Francesca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piero_della_Francesca) and the calm Virgins of Raphael are replaced by the troubled expressions of [Pontormo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontormo) and the emotional intensity of [El Greco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Greco).

17th century [Italian Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Baroque) painters include [Caravaggio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caravaggio), [Annibale Carracci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annibale_Carracci), [Artemisia Gentileschi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemisia_Gentileschi), [Mattia Preti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattia_Preti), [Carlo Saraceni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Saraceni) and [Bartolomeo Manfredi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeo_Manfredi). In the 18th century, French Rococo inspired the [Italian Rococo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Rococo_art) movement, with artists such as [Giovanni Battista Tiepolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo) and [Canaletto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaletto). [Antonio Canova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Canova)'s Italian Neoclassical sculpture focused on the idealist aspect of the movement.

&#x20;Some major Italian [Romantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_painting) painters from the 19th century were [Francesco Hayez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Hayez), [Giuseppe Bezzuoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Bezzuoli) and [Francesco Podesti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Podesti). [Impressionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism) was brought to Italy from France by the *[Macchiaioli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macchiaioli)* movement, led by [Giovanni Fattori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Fattori) and [Giovanni Boldini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Boldini), and [Realism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realism_\(arts\)) style, led by [Gioacchino Toma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gioacchino_Toma) and [Giuseppe Pellizza da Volpedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Pellizza_da_Volpedo). In the 20th century, Italian art was revolutionized by the [Futurism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futurism) movement, primarily through the sculptural works of [Umberto Boccioni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umberto_Boccioni) and [Giacomo Balla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Balla), and the metaphysical paintings of [Giorgio de Chirico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_de_Chirico), who influenced later [Surrealist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrealists) artists like [Bruno Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Caruso) and [Renato Guttuso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renato_Guttuso).

## Music

Main article: [Music of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Italy)



Pavarotti

History's most successful tenors, [Enrico Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Caruso) (above) and [Luciano Pavarotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Pavarotti) (below)



[Bartolomeo Cristofori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeo_Cristofori), the inventor of the [piano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piano)

Several instruments associated with [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_music), including the piano and violin, were invented in Italy; and many of the prevailing classical music forms, such as the [symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symphony), concerto, and [sonata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonata), can trace their roots back to innovations of 16th- and 17th-century Italian music. [Italian opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_opera) was founded in the early 17th century in cities such as [Mantua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantua) and [Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice), and has been influential on the Western opera tradition.[^cite_note-books.google.com-272]

Notable Italians composers include [Giovanni Pierluigi da Palestrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Pierluigi_da_Palestrina); [Claudio Monteverdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudio_Monteverdi); the [Baroque composers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Baroque_composers) [Scarlatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Scarlatti), [Corelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcangelo_Corelli), and [Vivaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Vivaldi); the [Classical composers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Classical_era_composers) [Paganini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niccol%C3%B2_Paganini) and [Rossini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gioachino_Rossini); and the [Romantic composers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Romantic-era_composers) [Bellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincenzo_Bellini), [Donizetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donizetti), [Verdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verdi), and [Puccini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puccini), whose operas, including *[La bohème](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_boh%C3%A8me)*, *[Tosca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosca)*, *[Madama Butterfly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madama_Butterfly)*, and *[Turandot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turandot)*, are among the most frequently worldwide performed in the [standard repertoire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_important_operas).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] In the Post Romantic era, the composer [Ottorino Respighi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottorino_Respighi) was also recognized internationally for his symphonic tone poems: *[ Pines of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pines_of_Rome)*, *[ Fountains of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fountains_of_Rome_\(symphonic_poem\))* and *[Roman Festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Festivals_\(Respighi\))*.[^cite_note-TRECCANI2016-275][^cite_note-276] Modern Italian composers such as [Berio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Berio), [Maderna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maderna), and [Nono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Nono_\(composer\)) proved significant in the development of [experimental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Experimental_music) and [electronic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music).

Starting in the late 19th century, Italians also emerged as notable [conductors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conductor_\(music\)) and musical directors of operatic and symphonic orchestras on the international concert hall stage. Included among them were: [Arturo Toscanini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arturo_Toscanini),[^cite_note-277] and [Victor de Sabata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_de_Sabata),[^cite_note-toscanini_letters-278]. In the modern era, several others followed their lead including: [Claudio Abbado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudio_Abbado),[^cite_note-Telegraph-279] [Riccardo Chailly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riccardo_Chailly),[^cite_note-280] [Carlo Maria Giulini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Maria_Giulini),[^cite_note-GUARD-281] [Fabio Luisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Luisi),[^cite_note-282] [Riccardo Muti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riccardo_Muti),[^cite_note-283] and [Gianandrea Noseda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianandrea_Noseda),[^cite_note-284]. Others achieved international acclaim as principal orchestral musicians including [Ernesto Köhler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_K%C3%B6hler),[^cite_note-Lorenzo-285] [Leonardo De Lorenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_De_Lorenzo).[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]

[Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) found a particularly strong foothold among Italians in the 1920s and remained popular despite the xenophobic cultural policies of the Fascist regime. Later, Italy embraced the [progressive rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_rock) movement of the 1970s, with bands like [PFM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premiata_Forneria_Marconi) and [Goblin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goblin_\(band\)), as well as [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco) and [electronic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music). [Italo disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italo_disco), characterised by a futuristic sound and prominent usage of synthesizers and [drum machines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_machine), was one of the earliest electronic dance genres and influenced [Euro disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro_disco). [Giorgio Moroder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Moroder), three-time [Academy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award) winner, was highly influential in the development of [EDM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music). Italian [pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_pop) is represented annually with the [Sanremo Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanremo_Music_Festival), which served as inspiration for the [Eurovision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision) song contest, and the [Festival of Two Worlds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festival_of_Two_Worlds) in [Spoleto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoleto). Singers such as pop [diva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diva) [Mina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mina_\(Italian_singer\)), classical crossover artist [Andrea Bocelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Bocelli), [Grammy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy) winner [Laura Pausini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Pausini), and European chart-topper [Eros Ramazzotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eros_Ramazzotti) have attained international acclaim.

## Theatre and dance

Main article: [Theatre of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_of_Italy)



[Carlo Goldoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Goldoni)

The traditions of [Italian theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_theatre) have their origins in the [ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greece) colonies of [Magna Graecia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magna_Graecia) in [Southern Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Italy)[^cite_note-288] and the theatre of the [Italic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italic_peoples) and [ancient Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_of_ancient_Rome).[^cite_note-289] During the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages), Italian theatre was expansive, comprising the dramatization of Catholic liturgies, the court performances of jesters, the songs of [troubadours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troubadour), and public city festivals.[^cite_note-290] [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance) theatre was marked by a resurgence of the classics; ancient theatrical texts were re-discovered, translated from Latin to Italian, and performed. The cities of [Ferrara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrara) and [Rome](25458.md) played a prominent role in the rediscovery and renewal of theatrical art in the fifteenth century.[^cite_note-291]



[Dario Fo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dario_Fo), one of the most widely performed playwrights in modern theatre, received international acclaim for his highly [improvisational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improvisational_theatre) style.

[^cite_note-292]

[^cite_note-293]

&#x20;He was awarded the [Nobel Prize for Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_for_Literature) in 1997.

[^cite_note-294]

From the 16th to 18th century, *[commedia dell'arte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commedia_dell'arte)* was a popular form of [improvisational theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improvisational_theatre).[^cite_note-\:0-295] Traveling troupes of players would set up an outdoor stage and provide entertainment in the form of [juggling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juggling), [acrobatics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acrobatics), and humorous plays called *[canovaccio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canovaccio).[^cite_note-\:0-295]* Actors improvised the performance from loose scenarios called [lazzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lazzi), that provided the basic situation and plot. Actors relied on a repertoire of [stock characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_character), such as foolish old men, devious servants, or military officers full of false [bravado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bravery).[^cite_note-\:0-295]

Noteworthy Italian theater actors and playwrights are [Jacopone da Todi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacopone_da_Todi), [Angelo Beolco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelo_Beolco), [Isabella Andreini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isabella_Andreini), [Carlo Goldoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Goldoni), [Eduardo Scarpetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_Scarpetta), [Ettore Petrolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ettore_Petrolini) [Eleonora Duse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleonora_Duse), [Eduardo De Filippo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_De_Filippo), [Carmelo Bene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmelo_Bene) and [Giorgio Strehler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Strehler).

&#x20;The [ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballet) dance genre also originated in Italy. It began during the Italian Renaissance as a form of entertainment at court weddings.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] At first, ballets were woven into the midst of an opera, providing the audience a moment of relief from the opera's dramatic intensity. By the 17th century, Italian ballets were performed in their entirety in between the acts of an opera, and had become a popular dance form in their own right by the 1800s.[^cite_note-298]

## Cinema

Main articles: [Cinema of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Italy), [List of Italian film directors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_film_directors), and [List of Italian actors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_actors)



[Federico Fellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Fellini), considered one of the most influential and widely revered [filmmakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmmaking) in the history of cinema

[^cite_note-299]

The history of [Italian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Italy) began in the late 1800s, a few months after the [Lumière brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auguste_and_Louis_Lumi%C3%A8re) started motion picture exhibitions.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] The first Italian director was [Vittorio Calcina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_Calcina), a collaborator of the Lumières, who filmed [Pope Leo XIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII) in 1896.[^cite_note-304] In the 1910s the Italian film industry developed rapidly.[^cite_note-treccani-cinematografia-305] *[Cabiria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabiria)*, a 1914 Italian [epic film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_film) directed by [Giovanni Pastrone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Pastrone), is considered the most famous Italian [silent film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_film).[^cite_note-treccani-cinematografia-305][^cite_note-306] It was also the first film in history to be shown in the [White House](33057.md).[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] The oldest European [avant-garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant-garde) cinema movement, [Italian futurism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Futurism_\(cinema\)), took place in the late 1910s.[^cite_note-310] After a period of decline in the 1920s, the Italian film industry was revitalized in the 1930s with the arrival of [sound film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_film). Popular Italian genres during this period were the [Telefoni Bianchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telefoni_Bianchi), consisting of comedies with glamorous backgrounds,[^cite_note-katz-311] and [Calligrafismo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calligrafismo), with its [artistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imitation_\(art\)), highly [formalistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formalism_\(art\)), and [expressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressionism) styling.[^cite_note-312]



[Vittorio De Sica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_De_Sica), one of the world's most acclaimed and influential filmmakers of all time

[^cite_note-313]



[Sergio Leone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Leone), widely regarded as one of the most influential directors in the history of cinema

[^cite_note-314]

[^cite_note-315]

Italian film was widely renowned after the end of [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-316] [Notable Italian film directors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_film_directors_from_Italy) from this period include [Vittorio De Sica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_De_Sica), [Federico Fellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Fellini), [Sergio Leone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Leone), [Pier Paolo Pasolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier_Paolo_Pasolini), [Luchino Visconti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luchino_Visconti), [Michelangelo Antonioni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo_Antonioni), [Dussio Tessari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duccio_Tessari), and [Roberto Rossellini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Rossellini); some of these are recognised among the greatest and most influential filmmakers of all time.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] Movies include world cinema treasures such as *[Bicycle Thieves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle_Thieves)*; *[La dolce vita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_dolce_vita)*; *[8½](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8%C2%BD)*; *[The Good, the Bad and the Ugly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Good,_the_Bad_and_the_Ugly)*; and *[Once Upon a Time in the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Once_Upon_a_Time_in_the_West)*.[^cite_note-319] Actresses such as [Sophia Loren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Loren), [Giulietta Masina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giulietta_Masina), and [Gina Lollobrigida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Lollobrigida) were popular during this period.[^cite_note-katz-311] A number of film genres were popularized by Italians during the 20th century, including [Peplum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peplum_film_genre), [Macaroni Combat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macaroni_Combat), [Musicarello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musicarello), [Poliziotteschi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poliziotteschi), [Commedia sexy all'italiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commedia_sexy_all'italiana), [Giallos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giallo), and the [Spaghetti Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spaghetti_Western).[^cite_note-Aulenti-320] Since the decline of Italian cinema in the 1980s, contemporary directors such as [Ermanno Olmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermanno_Olmi), [Bernardo Bertolucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardo_Bertolucci), [Giuseppe Tornatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Tornatore), [Gabriele Salvatores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriele_Salvatores), [Roberto Benigni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Benigni), [Matteo Garrone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Garrone), [Paolo Sorrentino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Sorrentino) and [Luca Guadagnino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_Guadagnino) brought critical acclaim back to Italian cinema.

The [Venice International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice_International_Film_Festival), held annually since 1932, is the oldest film festival in the world and one of the "[Big Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_festival#Notable_festivals)" alongside [Cannes](101460.md) and [Berlin](18948039.md).[^cite_note-VeniceFilmFest-321][^cite_note-322] Italy is the most awarded country at the [Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award) for [Best Foreign Language Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Foreign_Language_Film), with 14 awards won, 3 [Special Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Honorary_Award) and 28 [nominations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_submissions_for_the_Academy_Award_for_Best_Foreign_Language_Film).[^cite_note-323] As of 2016, Italian films have won 12 [Palmes d'Or](612946.md) (the second-most of any country),[^cite_note-324] 11 [Golden Lions](2386128.md)[^cite_note-325] and 7 [Golden Bears](21547166.md).[^cite_note-326]

## Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Italy) and [List of Italian inventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_inventions)



[Galileo Galilei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_Galilei), considered the "father" of [observational astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observational_astronomy),

[^cite_note-Clarendon-327]

&#x20;modern physics,

[^cite_note-Whitehouse-328]

&#x20;the [scientific method](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_method),

[^cite_note-Hobbes-329]

&#x20;and [modern science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_science)

[^cite_note-Disraeli-330]

Italians have contributed countless inventions and discoveries to various scientific fields. During the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance), Italian polymaths such as [Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci), [Michelangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo), and [Leon Battista Alberti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Battista_Alberti) made important contributions to including biology, architecture, and engineering. [Galileo Galilei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_Galilei), a physicist, mathematician, and astronomer, played a major role in the [Scientific Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_Revolution). His achievements include the invention of the thermometer and improvements to the [telescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telescope), which led to key astronomical observations and ultimately the triumph of [Copernicanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolaus_Copernicus) over the [Ptolemaic model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemaic_model). Other astronomers such as [Giovanni Domenico Cassini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Domenico_Cassini) and [Giovanni Schiaparelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Schiaparelli) made many important discoveries about the [Solar System](26903.md).

Prominent Italian biologists include:

- [Francesco Redi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Redi), who was the first to challenge the theory of [spontaneous generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spontaneous_generation) by demonstrating that maggots come from eggs of flies;

- [Marcello Malpighi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcello_Malpighi), who founded [microscopic anatomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microscopic_anatomy);

- [Lazzaro Spallanzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lazzaro_Spallanzani), who conducted important research in bodily functions, animal reproduction, and cellular theory;

- [Camillo Golgi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camillo_Golgi), who discovered the [Golgi complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golgi_complex) named after him and advanced understanding of the [neuron doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuron_doctrine);

- [Rita Levi-Montalcini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Levi-Montalcini), who discovered the [nerve growth factor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nerve_growth_factor);

- [Angelo Ruffini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelo_Ruffini), who first described the [Ruffini endings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruffini_endings) and was known for his work in [histology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Histology) and [embryology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embryology);

- [Filippo Pacini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filippo_Pacini), who discovered the [Pacinian corpuscles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacinian_corpuscles) and was the first to isolate the [cholera](7591.md) bacillus *[Vibrio cholerae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vibrio_cholerae)* in 1854, before [Robert Koch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Koch)'s more widely accepted discoveries 30 years later.



[Enrico Fermi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Fermi), builder of [the first nuclear reactor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Pile-1)

Prominent Italian scientists, engineers, and inventors include:

- [Amedeo Avogadro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amedeo_Avogadro), who was noted for his contributions to [molecular theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molecular_theory), in particular [Avogadro's law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avogadro's_law) and the [Avogadro constant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avogadro_constant);

- [Evangelista Torricelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelista_Torricelli), who invented the [barometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barometer);

- [Alessandro Volta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Volta), who invented the [electric battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_battery);

- [Guglielmo Marconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guglielmo_Marconi), who invented [radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio);

  [^cite_note-Hong-331]

- [Antonio Meucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Meucci), who developed a voice-communication apparatus, often considered the inventor of the first [telephone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telephone) before even [Alexander Graham Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Graham_Bell)),

  [^cite_note-guardian.co.uk-332]

  [^cite_note-333]

- [Galileo Ferraris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_Ferraris), who invented the first [induction motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Induction_motor) and pioneered the AC power system;

- [Eugenio Barsanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenio_Barsanti) and [Felice Matteucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felice_Matteucci), who invented the [internal combustion engine](41228673.md) in 1853.

In chemistry, [Giulio Natta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giulio_Natta), the inventor of the first catalyst for the production of isotactic propylene, received the 1963 Nobel prize for Chemistry along with [Karl Ziegler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Ziegler), for their work on high [polymers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymers).[^cite_note-334]

In physics, [Enrico Fermi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Fermi), a Nobel prize laureate, co-developed [quantum theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_mechanics) and led the team in Chicago that built the [first nuclear reactor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Pile-1).[^cite_note-335] A number of Italian physicists were forced to leave Italy in the 1930s by [Fascist laws against Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Racial_Laws), including Fermi, [Emilio G. Segrè](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_G._Segr%C3%A8) (who discovered the elements [technetium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technetium) and [astatine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astatine), and the [antiproton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiproton)),[^cite_note-336] and [Bruno Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Rossi) (a pioneer in cosmic rays and X-ray astronomy). Other notable Italian physicists include:

- [Ettore Majorana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ettore_Majorana), who theorized the [Majorana fermions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majorana_fermion);

  [^cite_note-337]

- [Giuseppe Occhialini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Occhialini), who received the [Wolf Prize in Physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Prize_in_Physics) for the discovery of the [pion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pion) or pi-[meson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meson) decay in 1947;

- [Carlo Rubbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Rubbia), who received the 1984 [Nobel Prize in Physics](52497.md) for work leading to the discovery of the [W and Z particles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W_and_Z_particles) at [CERN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERN);

  [^cite_note-338]

- [Giorgio Parisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Parisi), who received the 2021 [Nobel Prize in Physics](52497.md) for his work on [spin glasses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_glass).

  [^cite_note-339]

## Mathematics

Main article: [List of Italian mathematicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Italian_mathematicians)



[Gerolamo Cardano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerolamo_Cardano), one of the key figures in the foundation of [probability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Probability) and the earliest introducer of the [binomial coefficients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_coefficients) and the [binomial theorem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_theorem) in the Western world

During the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages), [Leonardo Fibonacci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Fibonacci) introduced the [Hindu–Arabic numeral system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu%E2%80%93Arabic_numeral_system) to the [Western world](21208200.md) and invented the [Fibonacci sequence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fibonacci_sequence).[^cite_note-340] [Gerolamo Cardano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerolamo_Cardano) established the foundation of [probability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Probability) and introduced the [binomial coefficients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_coefficients) and the [binomial theorem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomial_theorem); he also invented several mechanical devices. During the Renaissance, [Luca Pacioli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_Pacioli) introduced [accounting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accounting) to the world, publishing the first work on [double-entry bookkeeping system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-entry_bookkeeping_system). [Galileo Galilei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_Galilei) made several significant advances in mathematics. [Bonaventura Cavalieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonaventura_Cavalieri)'s works partially anticipated [integral calculus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_calculus) and popularized [logarithms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logarithms) in Italy.

[Jacopo Riccati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacopo_Riccati) invented the [Riccati equation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riccati_equation). [Maria Gaetana Agnesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Gaetana_Agnesi), the first woman to write a mathematics handbook, become the first woman mathematics professor at a university. [Gian Francesco Malfatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gian_Francesco_Malfatti) posed a famous geometry problem, the solution to which is now known as [Malfatti circles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malfatti_circles). [Paolo Ruffini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Ruffini_\(mathematician\)) is credited for his innovative work in mathematics, creating [Ruffini's rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruffini's_rule) and co-creating the [Abel–Ruffini theorem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abel%E2%80%93Ruffini_theorem). [Joseph-Louis Lagrange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph-Louis_Lagrange), who was one of the most influential mathematicians of his time, made essential contributions to [analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Analysis), [number theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Number_theory), and both [classical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_mechanics) and [celestial mechanics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celestial_mechanics).

[Gregorio Ricci-Curbastro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorio_Ricci-Curbastro) invented [tensor calculus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tensor_calculus) and [absolute differential calculus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_differential_calculus), which were popularized in a work he co-wrote with [Tullio Levi-Civita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tullio_Levi-Civita), and used in the development of the [theory of relativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theory_of_relativity). Ricci-Curbastro also wrote meaningful works on algebra, infinitesimal analysis, and papers on the theory of [real numbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_number).[^cite_note-341] [Giuseppe Peano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Peano), was a founder of [mathematical logic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathematical_logic) and [set theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Set_theory); alongside [John Venn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Venn), he drew the first [Venn diagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venn_diagram). [Beniamino Segre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beniamino_Segre) is one of the major contributors to [algebraic geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_geometry) and one of the founders of [finite geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finite_geometry). [Ennio De Giorgi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ennio_De_Giorgi), a [Wolf Prize in Mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Prize_in_Mathematics) recipient in 1990, solved [Bernstein's problem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernstein's_problem) about [minimal surfaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimal_surface) and the [19th Hilbert problem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilbert's_nineteenth_problem) on the regularity of solutions of [elliptic partial differential equations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_partial_differential_equations).+

## Nobel Prizes



[Ernesto Teodoro Moneta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Teodoro_Moneta) was awarded the [Nobel Peace Prize](26230922.md) in 1907. He adopted the motto *In varietate unitas!* which later inspired [Motto of the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motto_of_the_European_Union).



[Luigi Pirandello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Pirandello). He was awarded the 1934 [Nobel Prize in Literature](23385442.md). Pirandello's [tragic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tragedy) [farces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farce) are often seen as forerunners of the [Theatre of the Absurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_of_the_Absurd).



[Guglielmo Marconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guglielmo_Marconi), inventor and [electrical engineer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_engineering), known for his creation of a practical [radio wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_wave)-based [wireless telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wireless_telegraphy) system.

[^cite_note-342]

&#x20;This led to Marconi being credited as the [inventor of radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inventor_of_radio),

[^cite_note-343]

&#x20;and he won the 1909 [Nobel Prize in Physics](52497.md) "in recognition of their contributions to the development of wireless telegraphy".

[^cite_note-NPbio-344]
