## Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Italy)

See also: [List of World Heritage Sites in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Italy)



[Andrea Palladio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Palladio), one of the most influential individuals in the [history of architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_history)

Italy is home to [the greatest number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Sites_by_country) of [UNESCO](21786641.md) [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) (61 total) and half of the world's great art treasures.[^cite_note-368] Italians are known for their significant architectural achievements, such as the construction of arches, domes, and similar structures during [ancient Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome); the [Renaissance architectural movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_architecture) in the late-14th to 16th centuries; and [Palladianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palladianism), a style of construction that inspired the later [Neoclassical architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture) and [Italianate architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italianate_architecture) movements.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370]

During the [Fascist period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism), the [Novecento movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novecento_movement) flourished, with figures such as [Gio Ponti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gio_Ponti), [Pietro Aschieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Aschieri?action=edit\&redlink=1), and [Giovanni Muzio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Muzio). Fascist architecture (exemplified in the [EUR buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EUR,_Rome)) was followed by the Neo-liberty style, seen in earlier works of [Vittorio Gregotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_Gregotti), and [Brutalist architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brutalist_architecture), seen in the works of [Leonardo Savioli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_Savioli) and [Giancarlo De Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giancarlo_De_Carlo).

## Cuisine

Main article: [Italian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_cuisine)



[Bartolomeo Scappi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeo_Scappi)

[Italian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_cuisine) is a [Mediterranean cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_cuisine)[^cite_note-DavidRisotto-371] consisting of the [ingredients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingredient), [recipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recipe), and [cooking techniques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cooking_techniques) developed across the [Italian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Peninsula) since [antiquity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Roman_cuisine), and later spread around the world together with waves of [Italian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_diaspora).[^cite_note-Italian_Food-372][^cite_note-The_History_of_Italian_Cuisine_I-373][^cite_note-Thoms-374] Italian cuisine includes deeply rooted traditions common to the whole country, as well as [regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Italy) cuisines which are in continuous exchange.[^cite_note-Related_Articles-375][^cite_note-Indigo_Guide-376][^cite_note-Regional_Italian_Cuisine-377][^cite_note-Cronistoria_della_cucina_italiana-378][^cite_note-treccani.it-379]

One of the main characteristics of Italian cuisine is its simplicity, with many dishes made up of few ingredients, and therefore Italian cooks often rely on the quality of the ingredients, rather than the complexity of preparation.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] The most popular dishes and recipes, over the centuries, have often been created by ordinary people moreso than by [chefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chef), which is why many Italian recipes are suitable for home and daily [cooking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooking).[^cite_note-cibo360.it-382][^cite_note-gqitalia.it-383][^cite_note-Nonne_come_chef-384]

Noteworthy Italian chefs include [Bartolomeo Scappi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeo_Scappi), [Gualtiero Marchesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gualtiero_Marchesi), [Lidia Bastianich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lidia_Bastianich), [Antonio Carluccio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Carluccio), [Cesare Casella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cesare_Casella), [Carlo Cracco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Cracco), [Antonino Cannavacciuolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonino_Cannavacciuolo), [Gino D'Acampo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gino_D'Acampo), [Gianfranco Chiarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianfranco_Chiarini), [Massimiliano Alajmo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimiliano_Alajmo), [Massimo Bottura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimo_Bottura) and [Bruno Barbieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Barbieri).

## Fashion and design



[Guccio Gucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guccio_Gucci)

### Italian fashion

Main articles: [Italian fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_fashion) and [History of Italian fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Italian_fashion)

&#x20;Milan, Florence, and Rome are Italy's main [fashion capitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_capital).[^cite_note-385] Although most of the oldest Italian couturiers are based in Rome, Milan is seen as the fashion capital of Italy because many well-known designers are based there and it is the venue for the Italian designer collections. Major Italian fashion labels, such as [Gucci](844136.md), [Armani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armani), [Prada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prada), [Versace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versace), [Curiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curiel), [Valentino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentino_SpA), [Dolce & Gabbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolce_&_Gabbana), [Missoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoni), [Fendi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fendi), [Moschino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moschino), [Max Mara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Mara), [Trussardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trussardi), [Benetton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benetton_Group), and [Ferragamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferragamo), to name a few, are regarded among the finest fashion houses in the world. Accessory and jewelry labels, such as [Bulgari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgari), [Luxottica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxottica), and [Buccellati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buccellati) were founded in Italy and are internationally acclaimed. The fashion magazine [Vogue Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_Italia) is considered one of the most prestigious fashion magazines in the world.[^cite_note-386]&#x20;



[Stefano Gabbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefano_Gabbana) (*left*) and [Domenico Dolce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domenico_Dolce) (*right*)

Notable Italian fashion designers include [Guccio Gucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guccio_Gucci), [Salvatore Ferragamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvatore_Ferragamo), [Giorgio Armani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgio_Armani), [Gianni Versace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianni_Versace), [Valentino](919815.md), [Ottavio Missoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottavio_Missoni), [Nicola Trussardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicola_Trussardi), [Mariuccia Mandelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariuccia_Mandelli), [Rocco Barocco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocco_Barocco), [Roberto Cavalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Cavalli), [Renato Balestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renato_Balestra), [Laura Biagiotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Biagiotti), [Stefano Gabbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefano_Gabbana) and [Domenico Dolce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domenico_Dolce).

### Italian design

Main article: [Italian design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_design)

Italy is also prominent [in the field of design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_design), notably in interior design, architectural design, [industrial design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_design), and urban design.[^cite_note-Insight_Guides_(2004)-387] The country has produced some well-known furniture designers, such as [Gio Ponti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gio_Ponti) and [Ettore Sottsass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ettore_Sottsass), and Italian phrases such as *Bel Disegno* and *Linea Italiana* have entered the vocabulary of furniture design.[^cite_note-388] Examples of classic pieces of Italian [white goods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_goods) and pieces of furniture include [Zanussi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zanussi)'s washing machines and fridges,[^cite_note-Insight_Guides_(2004)-387] the "New Tone" sofas by Atrium,[^cite_note-Insight_Guides_(2004)-387] and the post-modern bookcase by Ettore Sottsass, inspired by Bob Dylan's song "[Stuck Inside of Mobile with the Memphis Blues Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuck_Inside_of_Mobile_with_the_Memphis_Blues_Again)".[^cite_note-Insight_Guides_(2004)-387] Milan and Turin are the nation's leaders in architectural design and industrial design. Milan hosts the [FieraMilano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FieraMilano), Europe's biggest design fair,[^cite_note-389] the *Fuori Salone*, and the [Salone del Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salone_del_Mobile), and has been home to the designers [Bruno Munari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Munari), [Lucio Fontana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucio_Fontana), [Enrico Castellani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Castellani), and [Piero Manzoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piero_Manzoni).[^cite_note-390]

## Sport

Main article: [Sport in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Italy)

663 Italian athletes have won medals at the Olympic games – 549 medals at the [Summer Olympics](27566.md) and 114 medals at the [Winter Olympics](34069.md) – which makes them the 6th most successful ethnic group in Olympic history. Italy consistently performs well in [swordsmanship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swordsmanship) events and [skiing](28478.md), thanks to the presence of the [Alps](981.md) and the [Apennines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apennines) in [Northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Italy) and [Central Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Italy).



[Edoardo Mangiarotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edoardo_Mangiarotti), the world's most successful [fencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fencing)

[^cite_note-391]

[Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_national_football_team) is one of the most successful national teams in association football, having won four [FIFA World Cups](11370.md), two UEFA European Championship, and one Olympic tournament. FIFA World Cup winners include [Giuseppe Meazza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Meazza), [Silvio Piola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Piola) (to date the highest goalscorer in Italian first league history), [Dino Zoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dino_Zoff), [Paolo Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Rossi), [Marco Tardelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Tardelli), [Bruno Conti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Conti), [Gianluigi Buffon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianluigi_Buffon), [Fabio Cannavaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Cannavaro), [Alessandro Del Piero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alessandro_Del_Piero), [Andrea Pirlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Pirlo), and [Francesco Totti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Totti). European champions include [Gianni Rivera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianni_Rivera), [Luigi Riva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luigi_Riva) (to date Italy's leading scorer of all time), [Sandro Salvadore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandro_Salvadore), [Giacomo Bulgarelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Bulgarelli), [Pietro Anastasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Anastasi), and [Giacinto Facchetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacinto_Facchetti). At the club level, Italy has won a total of 12 [European Cup / Champions' Leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Cup), 9 [UEFA Cups / UEFA Europa League](232175.md), and 7 [UEFA Cup Winners' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners'_Cup). Prominent players who achieved success at club level include [Giampiero Boniperti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giampiero_Boniperti), [Romeo Benetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romeo_Benetti), [Roberto Boninsegna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Boninsegna), [Roberto Bettega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Bettega), [Roberto Baggio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Baggio) and [Paolo Maldini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Maldini).

Italians have won more [World Cycling Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Cycling_Championship) than any other country except for [Belgium](3343.md). The [Giro d'Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giro_d'Italia) is a world-famous long-distance cycling race held every May, and constitutes one of the three [Grand Tours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Tour_\(cycling\)), along with the [Tour de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_de_France) and the [Vuelta a España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuelta_a_Espa%C3%B1a).

Italian professional [tennis](29773.md) players are almost always in the top 100 world ranking of male and female players. [Beach tennis with paddle racquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_tennis#Beach_tennis_with_paddle_racket) was invented by Italians, and is practised by many people across the country.

The [Italian national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_national_basketball_team)'s best results were gold at [Eurobasket 1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobasket_1983) and [EuroBasket 1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroBasket_1999), as well as silver at the Olympics in [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_2004_Summer_Olympics). [Lega Basket Serie A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lega_Basket_Serie_A) is widely considered one of the most competitive in Europe.[^cite_note-392]

The [Italian Volleyball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Volleyball_League) is regarded as the most difficult volleyball league in the world. The [male](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy_national_volleyball_team) and [female](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy_women's_national_volleyball_team) national teams often rank in the top 4 teams in the world.

[Rugby union](25405.md) was imported from France in the 1910s and has been regularly played since the 1920s. By the 1990s, when the [Italian national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy_national_rugby_union_team) managed to beat historically dominant teams like [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_rugby_union_team), [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland_national_rugby_union_team), and [France](955656.md), Italy gained admission to the Five Nation Championship, which had to be renamed [Six Nations](26547.md) as a result. Italy has taken part in the [Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup) since its inauguration in 1987 and never missed an edition, although to date it has never progressed past the group stage.



[Giacomo Agostini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Agostini), the most successful motorcyclist in the history of the World Championship

[^cite_note-oasport-393]

Some other notable Italian athletes include:

- Motorcycle racers such as [Giacomo Agostini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Agostini) and [Valentino Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentino_Rossi);

- [Federica Pellegrini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federica_Pellegrini), one of the few female swimmers to have set world records in more than one event;

- [Jessica Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessica_Rossi), who scored a [Shooting sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_sport) world record of 99;

- [Bruno Sammartino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Sammartino), a wrestler who held the [WWWF World Heavyweight Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WWE_Championship) for over 11 years across two reigns, the first of which was the longest single reign in the promotion's history

## Women

Main article: [Women in Italy](25794517.md)



[Samantha Cristoforetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Cristoforetti)

Famous women from Italy include actresses [Anna Magnani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Magnani), [Sofia Loren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia_Loren), and [Gina Lollobrigida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Lollobrigida); soprano [Renata Tebaldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renata_Tebaldi); ballet dancer [Carla Fracci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carla_Fracci); costume designer [Milena Canonero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milena_Canonero); athletes [Sara Simeoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Simeoni), [Deborah Compagnoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deborah_Compagnoni), [Valentina Vezzali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentina_Vezzali), and [Federica Pellegrini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federica_Pellegrini); writers [Natalia Ginzburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalia_Ginzburg), [Elsa Morante](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elsa_Morante), [Alda Merini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alda_Merini), and [Oriana Fallaci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriana_Fallaci); architect [Gae Aulenti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gae_Aulenti); scientist and 1986 Nobel Prize winner [Rita Levi-Montalcini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Levi-Montalcini); astrophysicist [Margherita Hack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margherita_Hack); astronaut [Samantha Cristoforetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Cristoforetti); pharmacologist [Elena Cattaneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elena_Cattaneo); [CERN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERN) Director-General [Fabiola Gianotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabiola_Gianotti); and politicians [Nilde Iotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilde_Iotti), [Tina Anselmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tina_Anselmi), [Emma Bonino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Bonino), and [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md), the first female [Prime Minister of Italy](273741.md).
